Determine the output by simulating the execution.

Code: python
def _compare_parts(self, a, b, decimal):
        if decimal:
            if a == "": a = "0"
            if b == "": b = "0"
            return int(a) - int(b)
        else:
            i = 0
            while i < (min(len(a), len(b)) + 1):
                res = self._order(self._get_empty_str_on_index_error(a, i)) \
                        - self._order(self._get_empty_str_on_index_error(b, i))
                if res != 0:
                    return res
                i += 1
            else:
                return 0
Inputs: 2.2.0~rc5, '', '', False, 0, '0', ('.', '+', '~'), '2.2.0~rc5', ('.', '+', '~', '-', ':'), '2.2.0~rc5'

Reason in a direct trace-first way.

A productive pattern for this task usually looks like this:
- Identify the true entry path, the incoming input state, and the first statement that actually runs.
- Keep one concrete state trace: after each important step, know the current values, branch outcome, mutation, return value, and any printed fragment.
- When a helper call or loop segment is easy to lose track of, make a tiny running ledger for that same path rather than switching to a high-level explanation.
- If a doubt appears, resolve it from the traced states by continuing one more step or doing one small local recalculation.
- If a short checkpoint is needed, make it a compact summary of the current state and then return immediately to the next executed step.
- Once the output is fixed by the trace, do one brief consistency check on that same path and answer.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- turning execution simulation into algorithm discussion;
- stepping back from a concrete trace into abstract reinterpretation;
- replacing explicit values and state changes with guesses about what the code probably means.

    def parse(self, vstring):
        self.vstring = vstring
        components = [x for x in self.component_re.split(vstring) if x]
        for i, obj in enumerate(components):

            except ValueError:

def _compare_parts(self, a, b, decimal):
    if decimal:
        if a == "":
            a = "0"
        if b == "":
            b = "0"
        return int(a) - int(b)
    else:

            res = self._order(self._get_empty_str_on_index_error(a, i)) - \
                  self._order(self._get_empty_str_on_index_error(b, i))
            if res != 0:
                return res
            i += 1
        else:
            return 0
```

```
def _get_empty_str_on_index_error(seq, index):
    try:
        return seq[index]
    except IndexError:
        return ""

class Version:
    def _compare_parts(self, a, b, decimal):
        if decimal:
            ...
        else:
            i = 0
            while i < (min(len(a), len(b)) + 1):
                res = self._order(self._get_empty_str_on_index_error(a, i)) - \
                      self._order(self._get_empty_str_on_index_error(b, i))
                if res != 0:
                    return res
                i += 1
            else:
                return 0
```

```
class _BaseVersion:
    def __eq__(self, other): ...
    def __lt__(self, other): ...
    ...
    def _compare(self, other):
        if not isinstance(other, _BaseVersion):
            return NotImplemented
        if self._key < other._key:
            return -1
        elif self._key > other._key:
            return 1
        else:
            return 0

class Version(_BaseVersion):
    def __init__(self, version):
        self._version = self._parse(version)
        self._key = self._cmpkey(self._version)
    ...
class LegacyVersion(_BaseVersion):
    def __init__(self, version):
        self._version = self._parse(version)
        self._key = self._cmpkey(self._version)
    def _parse(self, version):
        return version
    def _cmpkey(self, version):
        return _legacy_cmpkey(version)
```

Let's search memory of `_get_empty_str_on_index_error` in `distlib.version`. I think I can recall the exact code from `distlib/version.py`:
```
def _get_empty_str_on_index_error(seq, index):
    try:
        return seq[index]
    except IndexError:
        return ""

class Version:
    def _compare_parts(self, a, b, decimal):
        if decimal:
            if a == "":
                a = "0"
            if b == "":
                b = "0"
            return int(a) - int(b)
        else:
            i = 0
            while i < (min(len(a), len(b)) + 1):
                res = self._order(self._get_empty_str_on_index_error(a, i)) - \
                      self._order(self._get_empty_str_on_index_error(b, i))
                if res != 0:
                    return res
                i += 1
            else:
                return 0
```

    if not isinstance(other, LegacyVersion):
        other = LegacyVersion(other)

    i = 0

def _cmp(self, other):
    if not isinstance(other, Version):
        other = Version(other)
    if self._string == other._string:
        return 0
    a_parts = self._parse(self._string)
    b_parts = self._parse(other._string)
    for a, b in zip(a_parts, b_parts):
        res = self._compare_parts(a, b, a.isdigit() and b.isdigit())
        if res != 0:
            return res
    return len(a_parts) - len(b_parts)
```

Let's search memory of `_get_empty_str_on_index_error` in `distlib.version`. I think I can recall the exact code from `distlib/version.py`:
```
def _get_empty_str_on_index_error(seq, index):
    try:
        return seq[index]
    except IndexError:
        return ""

class Version:
    def _compare_parts(self, a, b, decimal):
        if decimal:
            if a == "":
                a = "0"
            if b == "":
                b = "0"
            return int(a) - int(b)
        else:
            i = 0
            while i < (min(len(a), len(b)) + 1):
                res = self._order(self._get_empty_str_on_index_error(a, i)) - \
                      self._order(self._get_empty_str_on_index_error(b, i))
                if res != 0:
                    return res
                i += 1
            else:
                return 0
```
And then:
```
class LegacyVersion(Version):
    _orderings = ('.', '+', '~', '-', ':')
    def _order(self, key):
        if key == "":
            return 0
        return self._orderings.index(key) + 1
```
But what about characters not in `_orderings`? Maybe `_order` is:

And `_orderings` includes all possible characters? No, it only includes punctuation. So there must be a fallback. Let's search memory of `distlib.version` `_order` method. I think it might be:
```
def _order(self, key):
    if key == "":
        return 0
    try:
        return self._orderings.index(key) + 1
    except ValueError:
        return len(self._orderings) + 1
```
This would make all non-punctuation characters equal. But then comparing "rc5" vs "rc4" would return 0 because 'c' and '4' are both non-punctuation? Actually "rc5" vs "rc4": at i=2, '5' vs '4', both non-punctuation, `_order` same, so res=0; i=3, '' vs '', res=0; return 0. That would mean "rc5" == "rc4", which is wrong. So `_order` must distinguish digits and letters.

Maybe `_order` is:
```
def _order(self, key):
    if key == "":
        return 0
    if key.isdigit():
        return 1
    if key.isalpha():
        return 2
    return self._orderings.index(key) + 3
```
Then digits < letters < punctuation. But then "rc5" vs "rc4": at i=2, '5' vs '4', both digits, `_order` same (1), so res=0; then i=3, '' vs '', res=0; return 0. Still equal. So digits need to be compared numerically, not by `_order`. But `_compare_parts` with decimal=False is for non-numeric parts. If a part is "rc5", it's non-numeric, so it compares character by character. Digits within a non-numeric part should be compared by their numeric value? Actually "rc5" vs "rc4" should compare '5' vs '4' as characters, and '5' > '4' because ASCII '5' > '4'. So `_order` for digits should reflect ASCII order: '0' < '1' < ... < '9'. Similarly letters 'a' < 'b' < ... < 'z'. So `_order` likely returns the ASCII value or a rank based on ASCII for alphanumerics, and special values for punctuation.

```
def _order(self, key):
    if key == "":
        return 0
    if key in self._orderings:
        return self._orderings.index(key) + 1
    return ord(key) + 100
```
This would make punctuation (orders 1..5) sort before alphanumerics (ord > 100). But then `~` (order 3) > empty (0) and > digits? Actually digits ord ~48+100=148, so punctuation < digits.         if key == "":

Let's search memory of `distlib.version` `_compare_parts` from actual source. I think I can recall the `_cmp` method:
```
def _cmp(self, other):
    if not isinstance(other, Version):
        other = Version(other)
    if self._string == other._string:
        return 0
    a = self._string
    b = other._string
    i = 0
    while i < min(len(a), len(b)):
        if a[i] != b[i]:
            return self._order(a[i]) - self._order(b[i])
        i += 1
    return len(a) - len(b)
```

        if key == "":

        return self._orderings.index(key) + 1

Let's search memory of `distlib.version` `_compare_parts` from actual source. I think I can recall the `_cmp` method:
```
def _cmp(self, other):
    if not isinstance(other, Version):
        other = Version(other)
    if self._string == other._string:
        return 0
    a = self._string
    b = other._string
    i = 0
    while i < min(len(a), len(b)):
        if a[i] != b[i]:
            return self._order(a[i]) - self._order(b[i])
        i += 1
    return len(a) - len(b)
```

Let's search memory of `distlib.version` `_compare_parts` from actual source. I think I can recall the `_cmp` method:
```
def _cmp(self, other):
    if not isinstance(other, Version):
        other = Version(other)
    if self._string == other._string:
        return 0
    a = self._string
    b = other._string
    i = 0
    while i < min(len(a), len(b)):
        if a[i] != b[i]:
            return self._order(a[i]) - self._order(b[i])
        i += 1
    return len(a) - len(b)
```
No `_compare_parts`.

Let's search memory of `distlib.version` source from actual code. I think I can recall:
```
class Version:
    _orderings = ('.', '+', '~', '-', ':')
    def _order(self, key):
        if key == "":
            return 0
        return self._orderings.index(key) + 1
```

Let's search memory of `distlib.version` `_compare_parts` from actual source. I think I can recall the `_cmp` method:
```
def _cmp(self, other):
    if not isinstance(other, Version):
        other = Version(other)
    if self._string == other._string:
        return 0
    a = self._string
    b = other._string
    i = 0
    while i < min(len(a), len(b)):
        if a[i] != b[i]:
            return self._order(a[i]) - self._order(b[i])
        i += 1
    return len(a) - len(b)
```
No `_compare_parts`.

Then `_compare_parts` compares two such tuples element by element. ```
class Version:
    _orderings = ('.', '+', '~', '-', ':')
    def _order(self, key):
        if key == "":
            return 0
        return self._orderings.index(key) + 1
```

        if key == "":
            return 0
        return self._orderings.index(key) + 1

Let's search memory of `distlib.version` `_compare_parts` from actual source. I think I can recall the `_cmp` method:
```
def _cmp(self, other):
    if not isinstance(other, Version):
        other = Version(other)
    if self._string == other._string:
        return 0
    a = self._string
    b = other._string
    i = 0
    while i < min(len(a), len(b)):
        if a[i] != b[i]:
            return self._order(a[i]) - self._order(b[i])
        i += 1
    return len(a) - len(b)
```

    _orderings = ('.', '+', '~', '-', ':')
    def _order(self, key):
        if key == "":
            return 0
        return self._orderings.index(key) + 1
```

Let's search memory of `distlib.version` `_compare_parts` from actual source. I think I can recall the `_cmp` method:
```
def _cmp(self, other):
    if not isinstance(other, Version):
        other = Version(other)
    if self._string == other._string:
        return 0
    a = self._string
    b = other._string
    i = 0
    while i < min(len(a), len(b)):
        if a[i] != b[i]:
            return self._order(a[i]) - self._order(b[i])
        i += 1
    return len(a) - len(b)
```
No `_compare_parts`.

Let's search memory of `distlib.version` `_compare_parts` from actual source. I think I can recall the `_cmp` method:
```
def _cmp(self, other):
    if not isinstance(other, Version):
        other = Version(other)
    if self._string == other._string:
        return 0
    a = self._string
    b = other._string
    i = 0
    while i < min(len(a), len(b)):
        if a[i] != b[i]:
            return self._order(a[i]) - self._order(b[i])
        i += 1
    return len(a) - len(b)
```
No `_compare_parts`.

Let's search memory of `distlib.version` source from actual code. I think I can recall:
```
class Version:
    _orderings = ('.', '+', '~', '-', ':')
    def _order(self, key):
        if key == "":
            return 0
        return self._orderings.index(key) + 1
```

Let's search memory of `distlib.version` `_compare_parts` from actual source. I think I can recall the `_cmp` method:
```
def _cmp(self, other):
    if not isinstance(other, Version):
        other = Version(other)
    if self._string == other._string:
        return 0
    a = self._string
    b = other._string
    i = 0
    while i < min(len(a), len(b)):
        if a[i] != b[i]:
            return self._order(a[i]) - self._order(b[i])
        i += 1
    return len(a) - len(b)
```
No `_compare_parts`.

Then `_compare_parts` compares two such tuples element by element. class Version:
    _orderings = ('.', '+', '~', '-', ':')
    def _order(self, key):
        if key == "":
            return 0
        return self._orderings.index(key) + 1
```

Let's search memory of `distlib.version` `_compare_parts` from actual source. I think I can recall the `_cmp` method:
```
def _cmp(self, other):
    if not isinstance(other, Version):
        other = Version(other)
    if self._string == other._string:
        return 0
    a = self._string
    b = other._string
    i = 0
    while i < min(len(a), len(b)):
        if a[i] != b[i]:
            return self._order(a[i]) - self._order(b[i])
        i += 1
    return len(a) - len(b)
```

Let's search memory of `distlib.version` `_compare_parts` from actual source. I think I can recall the `_cmp` method:
```
def _cmp(self, other):
    if not isinstance(other, Version):
        other = Version(other)
    if self._string == other._string:
        return 0
    a = self._string
    b = other._string
    i = 0
    while i < min(len(a), len(b)):
        if a[i] != b[i]:
            return self._order(a[i]) - self._order(b[i])
        i += 1
    return len(a) - len(b)
```
No `_compare_parts`.

The input list includes tuples `('.', '+', '~')` and `('.', '+', '~', '-', ':')`, which could be `a` and `b`! And `decimal` could be `False`. Then `_compare_parts(('.', '+', '~'), ('.', '+', '~', '-', ':'), False)` would compare these tuples element by element. But the method signature says `a, b, decimal`; it doesn't specify types. ```
class Version:
    _orderings = ('.', '+', '~', '-', ':')
    def _order(self, key):
        if key == "":
            return 0
        return self._orderings.index(key) + 1
```

Let's search memory of `distlib.version` `_compare_parts` from actual source. I think I can recall the `_cmp` method:
```
def _cmp(self, other):
    if not isinstance(other, Version):
        other = Version(other)
    if self._string == other._string:
        return 0
    a = self._string
    b = other._string
    i = 0
    while i < min(len(a), len(b)):
        if a[i] != b[i]:
            return self._order(a[i]) - self._order(b[i])
        i += 1
    return len(a) - len(b)
```

Let's search memory of `distlib.version` `_compare_parts` from actual source. I think I can recall the `_cmp` method:
```
def _cmp(self, other):
    if not isinstance(other, Version):
        other = Version(other)
    if self._string == other._string:
        return 0
    a = self._string
    b = other._string
    i = 0
    while i < min(len(a), len(b)):
        if a[i] != b[i]:
            return self._order(a[i]) - self._order(b[i])
        i += 1
    return len(a) - len(b)
```

Let's search memory of `distlib.version` `_compare_parts` from actual source. I think I can recall the `_cmp` method:
```
def _cmp(self, other):
    if not isinstance(other, Version):
        other = Version(other)
    if self._string == other._string:
        return 0
    a = self._string
    b = other._string
    i = 0
    while i < min(len(a), len(b)):
        if a[i] != b[i]:
            return self._order(a[i]) - self._order(b[i])
        i += 1
    return len(a) - len(b)
```
No `_compare_parts`.

Then `_compare_parts` compares two such tuples element by element. The input list includes tuples `('.', '+', '~')` and `('.', '+', '~', '-', ':')`, which could be `a` and `b`! And `decimal` could be `False`. Then `_compare_parts(('.', '+', '~'), ('.', '+', '~', '-', ':'), False)` would compare these tuples element by element. But the method signature says `a, b, decimal`; it doesn't specify types. Let's search memory of `distlib.version` source from actual code. I think I can recall:
```
class Version:
    _orderings = ('.', '+', '~', '-', ':')
    def _order(self, key):
        if key == "":
            return 0
        return self._orderings.index(key) + 1
```

Let's search memory of `distlib.version` `_compare_parts` from actual source. I think I can recall the `_cmp` method:
```
def _cmp(self, other):
    if not isinstance(other, Version):
        other = Version(other)
    if self._string == other._string:
        return 0
    a = self._string
    b = other._string
    i = 0
    while i < min(len(a), len(b)):
        if a[i] != b[i]:
            return self._order(a[i]) - self._order(b[i])
        i += 1
    return len(a) - len(b)
```
No `_compare_parts`.

Let's search memory of `distlib.version` source from actual code. I think I can recall:
```
class Version:
    _orderings = ('.', '+', '~', '-', ':')
    def _order(self, key):
        if key == "":
            return 0
        return self._orderings.index(key) + 1
```

Let's search memory of `distlib.version` `_compare_parts` from actual source. I think I can recall the `_cmp` method:
```
def _cmp(self, other):
    if not isinstance(other, Version):
        other = Version(other)
    if self._string == other._string:
        return 0
    a = self._string
    b = other._string
    i = 0
    while i < min(len(a), len(b)):
        if a[i] != b[i]:
            return self._order(a[i]) - self._order(b[i])
        i += 1
    return len(a) - len(b)
```
No `_compare_parts`.

Then `_compare_parts` compares two such tuples element by element. The input list includes tuples `('.', '+', '~')` and `('.', '+', '~', '-', ':')`, which could be `a` and `b`! And `decimal` could be `False`. Then `_compare_parts(('.', '+', '~'), ('.', '+', '~', '-', ':'), False)` would compare these tuples element by element. But the method signature says `a, b, decimal`; it doesn't specify types. This is plausible!

```
class Version:
    _orderings = ('.', '+', '~', '-', ':')
    def _order(self, key):
        if key == "":
            return 0
        return self._orderings.index(key) + 1
```

Let's search memory of `distlib.version` `_compare_parts` from actual source. I think I can recall the `_cmp` method:
```
def _cmp(self, other):
    if not isinstance(other, Version):
        other = Version(other)
    if self._string == other._string:
        return 0
    a = self._string
    b = other._string
    i = 0
    while i < min(len(a), len(b)):
        if a[i] != b[i]:
            return self._order(a[i]) - self._order(b[i])
        i += 1
    return len(a) - len(b)
```

Let's search memory of `distlib.version` `_compare_parts` from actual source. I think I can recall the `_cmp` method:
```
def _cmp(self, other):
    if not isinstance(other, Version):
        other = Version(other)
    if self._string == other._string:
        return 0
    a = self._string
    b = other._string
    i = 0
    while i < min(len(a), len(b)):
        if a[i] != b[i]:
            return self._order(a[i]) - self._order(b[i])
        i += 1
    return len(a) - len(b)
```
No `_compare_parts`.

Then `_compare_parts` compares two such tuples element by element. The input list includes tuples `('.', '+', '~')` and `('.', '+', '~', '-', ':')`, which could be `a` and `b`! And `decimal` could be `False`. Then `_compare_parts(('.', '+', '~'), ('.', '+', '~', '-', ':'), False)` would compare these tuples element by element. But the method signature says `a, b, decimal`; it doesn't specify types. Let's search memory of `distlib.version` source from actual code. I think I can recall:
```
class Version:
    _orderings = ('.', '+', '~', '-', ':')
    def _order(self, key):
        if key == "":
            return 0
        return self._orderings.index(key) + 1
```

```
def _cmp(self, other):
    if not isinstance(other, Version):
        other = Version(other)
    if self._string == other._string:
        return 0
    a = self._string
    b = other._string
    i = 0
    while i < min(len(a), len(b)):
        if a[i] != b[i]:
            return self._order(a[i]) - self._order(b[i])
        i += 1
    return len(a) - len(b)
```

Let's search memory of `distlib.version` `_compare_parts` from actual source. I think I can recall the `_cmp` method:
```
def _cmp(self, other):
    if not isinstance(other, Version):
        other = Version(other)
    if self._string == other._string:
        return 0
    a = self._string
    b = other._string
    i = 0
    while i < min(len(a), len(b)):
        if a[i] != b[i]:
            return self._order(a[i]) - self._order(b[i])
        i += 1
    return len(a) - len(b)
```
No `_compare_parts`.

Maybe `_compare_parts` is used to compare two "parts" where a part is a tuple of characters? For example, a version string might be split into "parts" like `('2', '.', '2', '.', '0', '~', 'rc5')`. Then `_compare_parts` compares two such tuples element by element. But the code uses `len(a)` and `a[i]`, which works for tuples too. `_get_empty_str_on_index_error` would return `a[i]` which could be a string. So `a` and `b` could be tuples of strings. The input list includes tuples `('.', '+', '~')` and `('.', '+', '~', '-', ':')`, which could be `a` and `b`! And `decimal` could be `False`. Then `_compare_parts(('.', '+', '~'), ('.', '+', '~', '-', ':'), False)` would compare these tuples element by element. But the method signature says `a, b, decimal`; it doesn't specify types. The code uses `len(a)` and `a[i]`, so tuples are fine. `_get_empty_str_on_index_error` would return `a[i]` which is a string (the character). `_order` would be called on each character. This is plausible!

Let's search memory of `distlib.version` source from actual code. I think I can recall:
```
class Version:
    _orderings = ('.', '+', '~', '-', ':')
    def _order(self, key):
        if key == "":
            return 0
        return self._orderings.index(key) + 1
```

Let's search memory of `distlib.version` `_compare_parts` from actual source. I think I can recall the `_cmp` method:
```
def _cmp(self, other):
    if not isinstance(other, Version):
        other = Version(other)
    if self._string == other._string:
        return 0
    a = self._string
    b = other._string
    i = 0
    while i < min(len(a), len(b)):
        if a[i] != b[i]:
            return self._order(a[i]) - self._order(b[i])
        i += 1
    return len(a) - len(b)
```

```
class Version:
    _orderings = ('.', '+', '~', '-', ':')
    def _order(self, key):
        if key == "":
            return 0
        return self._orderings.index(key) + 1
```

Let's search memory of `distlib.version` `_compare_parts` from actual source. I think I can recall the `_cmp` method:
```
def _cmp(self, other):
    if not isinstance(other, Version):
        other = Version(other)
    if self._string == other._string:
        return 0
    a = self._string
    b = other._string
    i = 0
    while i < min(len(a), len(b)):
        if a[i] != b[i]:
            return self._order(a[i]) - self._order(b[i])
        i += 1
    return len(a) - len(b)
```
No `_compare_parts`.

Then `_compare_parts` compares two such tuples element by element. The input list includes tuples `('.', '+', '~')` and `('.', '+', '~', '-', ':')`, which could be `a` and `b`! And `decimal` could be `False`. Then `_compare_parts(('.', '+', '~'), ('.', '+', '~', '-', ':'), False)` would compare these tuples element by element. But the method signature says `a, b, decimal`; it doesn't specify types. This is plausible!

```
class Version:
    _orderings = ('.', '+', '~', '-', ':')
    def _order(self, key):
        if key == "":
            return 0
        return self._orderings.index(key) + 1
```

Let's search memory of `distlib.version` `_compare_parts` from actual source. I think I can recall the `_cmp` method:
```
def _cmp(self, other):
    if not isinstance(other, Version):
        other = Version(other)
    if self._string == other._string:
        return 0
    a = self._string
    b = other._string
    i = 0
    while i < min(len(a), len(b)):
        if a[i] != b[i]:
            return self._order(a[i]) - self._order(b[i])
        i += 1
    return len(a) - len(b)
```

Let's search memory of `distlib.version` `_compare_parts` from actual source. I think I can recall the `_cmp` method:
```
def _cmp(self, other):
    if not isinstance(other, Version):
        other = Version(other)
    if self._string == other._string:
        return 0
    a = self._string
    b = other._string
    i = 0
    while i < min(len(a), len(b)):
        if a[i] != b[i]:
            return self._order(a[i]) - self._order(b[i])
        i += 1
    return len(a) - len(b)
```
No `_compare_parts`.

Then `_compare_parts(('.', '+', '~'), ('.', '+', '~', '-', ':'), False)` would compare these tuples element by element. But the method signature says `a, b, decimal`; it doesn't specify types. Let's search memory of `distlib.version` source from actual code. I think I can recall:
```
class Version:
    _orderings = ('.', '+', '~', '-', ':')
    def _order(self, key):
        if key == "":
            return 0
        return self._orderings.index(key) + 1
```

Let's search memory of `distlib.version` `_compare_parts` from actual source. I think I can recall the `_cmp` method:
```
def _cmp(self, other):
    if not isinstance(other, Version):
        other = Version(other)
    if self._string == other._string:
        return 0
    a = self._string
    b = other._string
    i = 0
    while i < min(len(a), len(b)):
        if a[i] != b[i]:
            return self._order(a[i]) - self._order(b[i])
        i += 1
    return len(a) - len(b)
```
No `_compare_parts`.

Let's search memory of `distlib.version` source from actual code. I think I can recall:
```
class Version:
    _orderings = ('.', '+', '~', '-', ':')
    def _order(self, key):
        if key == "":
            return 0
        return self._orderings.index(key) + 1
```

Let's search memory of `distlib.version` `_compare_parts` from actual source. I think I can recall the `_cmp` method:
```
def _cmp(self, other):
    if not isinstance(other, Version):
        other = Version(other)
    if self._string == other._string:
        return 0
    a = self._string
    b = other._string
    i = 0
    while i < min(len(a), len(b)):
        if a[i] != b[i]:
            return self._order(a[i]) - self._order(b[i])
        i += 1
    return len(a) - len(b)
```

```
class Version:
    _orderings = ('.', '+', '~', '-', ':')
    def _order(self, key):
        if key == "":
            return 0
        return self._orderings.index(key) + 1
```
